0\leq r<L,1\leq b\leq B
is bounded above in expectation by
\mathscr{L}_{j}
for all
0\leq j<T
, substituting this into (
103
) yields

\mathbb{E}^{1}\left[\max_{0\leq r<L}W_{2}^{2}(\hat{\nu}^{N,L}_{\frac{r}{L},\tau},\bar{\nu}^{N,L}_{\frac{r}{L},\tau})\right]\leq D_{1}\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\kappa^{0}_{j,\tau}\mathscr{L}_{j}.

Applying (
115
) to bound
\mathscr{L}_{\tau}
then gives

\max_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\mathbb{E}^{1}\left[\max_{0\leq r<L}W_{2}^{2}(\hat{\nu}^{N,L}_{\frac{r}{L},\tau},\bar{\nu}^{N,L}_{\frac{r}{L},\tau})\right]\leq D_{1}C_{9}\left(\frac{1}{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{L^{2/3}H}\right)\max_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\kappa^{0}_{j,\tau},\qquad\mathbb{P}^{0}-a.s.

Lemma
6
now follows by bounding the sum of
\kappa^{0}_{i,T}
defined in (
65
). By interchanging the order of summation, we obtain

\sum_{j=0}^{\tau-1}\kappa^{0}_{j,\tau}=\sum_{i=1}^{T}\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})\eta_{i}}{1-\beta_{1}^{i}}(\beta_{1}^{i-j-1}+2\beta_{2}^{i-j-1})=C_{\kappa}\sum_{i=1}^{\tau}\eta_{i},

where the geometric series is evaluated to give the constant
C_{\kappa}
defined in (
119
).

J.1
Existence of
\widetilde{J}

The existence of
\widetilde{J}
is clear when
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}
are either linear or independent of
z,w
. Therefore, the only problematic
f
are
f^{\gamma z}_{2},f_{1}^{\gamma\mu}
and
f^{\gamma\mu}_{2}
given in the previous section. For ease of notation, we shall write
A=\beta\theta_{K}^{T}\theta_{Q}
,
U=\theta_{O}^{T}\theta_{V}
and denote the indices of the
j
-th block of
d
components by
I_{j}=[(j-1)d+1:jd]
. We state the relevant
\widetilde{J}
in block form, where most of the blocks are identically zero.

Using Definition
32
, we see that the
J^{\gamma z}_{2}
corresponding to
f^{\gamma z}_{2}
in (
107
) satisfies Assumption
8
with
\widetilde{J}
given by

\displaystyle J^{\gamma z}_{2}(z,w,u,\theta)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widetilde{J}_{2}^{\gamma z},z\otimes w\otimes w\otimes u\right\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}},

\displaystyle(\widetilde{J}_{2}^{\gamma z})_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}A_{kl}\,U_{ij}&\text{if }i\in I_{2},\,j\in I_{1},\,k\in I_{1},\,l\in I_{1}\\
-A_{kl}\,U_{ij}&\text{if }i\in I_{2},\,j\in I_{1},\,k\in I_{3},\,l\in I_{1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

for
i\in[1:2d],j,k\in[1:4d]
and
l\in[1:d]
. Since
\langle\theta_{Q}w_{1},\theta_{K}z_{1}\rangle
is linear in both
z,w
, only the term
\langle\theta_{Q}w_{1},\theta_{K}w_{3}\rangle
in
f_{1}^{\gamma\mu}
, defined in (
108
), needs further study. Notice that it can be expressed as

\beta\langle\theta_{Q}w_{1},\theta_{K}w_{3}\rangle=\left\langle\widetilde{J}^{\gamma\mu}_{1,2},ww^{T}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad(\widetilde{J}_{1,2}^{\gamma\mu})_{ij}=\begin{cases}A_{ij}&\text{if }i\in I_{3},\,j\in I_{1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

for
i,j\in[1:4d]
. Thus,
f_{1}^{\gamma\mu}
is the sum of two terms that satisfy Assumption
8
. Likewise, the final term in
f_{2}^{\gamma\mu}
defined in (
108
) is linear in
w_{2}
and so doesn’t require further study. The other terms of
J_{2}^{\gamma\mu}
satisfy Assumption
8
with
\widetilde{J}
given by

\displaystyle\beta\langle\theta_{K}u,\theta_{Q}w_{1}\rangle\langle\theta_{V}z_{1},\theta_{O}w_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widetilde{J}^{\gamma\mu}_{2,2},z\otimes w^{\otimes 2}\otimes u\right\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}},

\displaystyle(\widetilde{J}_{2,2}^{\gamma z})_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}A_{lk}\,U_{ji}&\text{if }i\in I_{1},\,j\in I_{2},\,k\in I_{1},\,l\in I_{1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

\displaystyle-\beta\langle\theta_{K}u,\theta_{Q}w_{1}\rangle\langle\theta_{V}w_{3},\theta_{O}w_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widetilde{J}^{\gamma\mu}_{2,3},w^{\otimes 3}\otimes u\right\rangle_{\mathrm{HS}},

\displaystyle(\widetilde{J}_{2,3}^{\gamma z})_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-A_{lk}\,U_{ji}&\text{if }i\in I_{3},\,j\in I_{2},\,k\in I_{1},\,l\in I_{1}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

Appendix K
Preliminary Lemmas on AdamW

K.1
Properties of
\kappa_{i,T}

Lemma 41
.

Let
D\geq 0
,
\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,T}
be defined by (
66
), with
\beta_{1}\leq\beta_{2}
, and
(A_{j})_{j=0}^{T}
a non-negative sequence such that
A_{j}\geq(1-\eta_{j}\lambda)A_{j-1}
. Suppose
u_{j}
satisfies

u_{j}\leq A_{j}+D\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,j}u_{i},

(116)

for all
j\geq 0
, where by convention
\sum_{i=0}^{-1}=0
. Then, there exists a constant
\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{D}(\lambda,\beta_{1},\beta_{2},D)
such that

\displaystyle u_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\left(A_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{T}\left(A_{j}-(1-\eta_{j}\lambda)A_{j-1}\right)\right)exp\left[\widetilde{D}S_{T}\right],\qquad S_{T}=\sum_{j=0}^{T}\eta_{j}.

(117)

When
(A_{j})^{T}_{j=0}
is non-decreasing, the bound simplifies to
u_{T}\leq A_{T}(1+\lambda S_{T})\exp(\widetilde{D}S_{T})
.

Proof.

Let
v_{j}
be defined by the sequence that satisfies (
116
) with equality and
v_{0}=A_{0}
. Notice that
\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,T}
, defined in equation, (
66
) satisfies the recursion relationship

\kappa^{\lambda}_{j,T}=(1-\eta_{T}\lambda)\kappa^{\lambda}_{j,T-1}+\eta_{T}\frac{(1-\beta_{1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}}(\beta_{1}^{T-j-1}+2\beta_{2}^{T-j-1}),\qquad\kappa^{\lambda}_{T-1,T}=3\eta_{T}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}},

for any
j\in[0:T-2]
. Therefore, substituting this recursion formula into the series expression for
v_{T}
gives

\displaystyle v_{T}=A_{T}

\displaystyle+D\sum_{i=0}^{T-2}\left((1-\eta_{T}\lambda)\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,T-1}v_{i}+(1-\beta_{1})\frac{\eta_{T}(\beta_{1}^{T-i-1}+2\beta_{2}^{T-i-1})}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}}v_{i}\right)+3Dv_{T-1}\eta_{T}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}},

for
T\geq 1
. The second term is precisely
(1-\eta_{T}\lambda)(v_{T-1}-A_{T-1})
, and so

\displaystyle v_{T}

\displaystyle=A_{T}+(1-\eta_{T}\lambda)(v_{T-1}-A_{T-1})+D\eta_{T}(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\frac{\beta_{1}^{T-i-1}+2\beta_{2}^{T-i-1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}}v_{i}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta_{T}\left(DC_{\kappa}-\lambda)\mathbbm{1}_{DC_{\kappa}>0}\right)\max_{0\leq j\leq T-1}v_{j}+A_{T}-(1-\eta_{T}\lambda)A_{T-1},

(118)

having simplified the geometric series via the bound

\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\beta_{1}^{T-i-1}+2\beta_{2}^{T-i-1}\right)=1+2\frac{1-\beta_{2}^{T}}{1-\beta_{1}^{T}}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{2}}\leq 1+2B_{\beta}^{2}=:C_{\kappa},

(119)

where
B_{\beta}
is defined in Lemma
43
. Since either
v_{T}<v_{j}
for some
j\in[0:T-1]
, or
v_{T}
is bounded by equation (
118
), we obtain

\max_{0\leq j\leq T}v_{j}\leq(1+\eta_{T}\left(DC_{\kappa}-\lambda)\mathbbm{1}_{DC_{\kappa}>\lambda}\right)\max_{0\leq j\leq T-1}v_{j}+\left(A_{T}-(1-\eta_{T}\lambda)A_{T-1}\right),

for
T\geq 1
. Here, we have used the assumed inequality
A_{j}\geq(1-\eta_{j}\lambda)A_{j-1}
for every
j\in[1:T]
. Hence, using the convention
\prod_{i=T+1}^{T}A_{i}=1
and applying the discrete Grönwall inequality with
v_{0}=A_{0}
gives

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq i\leq T}v_{i}

\displaystyle\leq A_{0}\prod_{j=1}^{T}(1+\eta_{j}(DC_{\kappa}-\lambda)\mathbbm{1}_{DC_{\kappa}>\lambda})

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{T}\left(A_{l}-(1-\eta_{l}\lambda)A_{l-1}\right)\prod_{j=l+1}^{T}(1+\eta_{j}(DC_{\kappa}-\lambda)\mathbbm{1}_{DC_{\kappa}>\lambda})

\displaystyle\leq\left(A_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{T}\left(A_{j}-(1-\eta_{j}\lambda)A_{j-1}\right)\right)\exp\left[(DC_{\kappa}-\lambda)S_{T}\mathbbm{1}_{DC_{\kappa}>\lambda}\right],

where
S_{T}=\sum_{j=1}^{T}\eta_{j}
. If the sequence
(A_{j})_{j=0}^{T}
is non-decreasing, the telescoping sum simplifies to give

\max_{0\leq i\leq T}v_{i}\leq A_{T}(1+\lambda S_{T})\exp\left[(DC_{\kappa}-1)S_{T}\mathbbm{1}_{DC_{\kappa}>\lambda}\right].

By induction, we see that
u_{i}\leq v_{i}
for every
i\in[0:T]
and so
u_{T}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq T}v_{i}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 42
.

Take
\kappa^{\lambda}_{j,T}
defined by equation (
66
). Then, with
C_{\kappa}
defined in (
119
), we have that

\sum_{j=0}^{T-1}\kappa^{\lambda}_{j,T}\leq C_{\kappa}\lambda^{-1}.

Proof.

Recall the expression for
\kappa_{i,T}
given in (
65
). By interchanging the order of summation, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,T}=(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{T}\frac{\eta_{j}\alpha_{j+1,T}}{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}(\beta_{1}^{j-i-1}+2\beta_{2}^{j-i-1}).

Then, simplifying the geometric series via equation (
119
) gives

\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,T}=C_{\kappa}\sum_{j=1}^{T}\eta_{j}\alpha_{j+1,T}.

Since
-\eta_{j}\alpha_{j+1,T}=\lambda^{-1}(\alpha_{j,T}-\alpha_{j+1,T})
and
\alpha_{T+1,T}=1
by convention, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{T-1}\kappa^{\lambda}_{i,T}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\kappa}}{\lambda}\sum_{j=1}^{T}(\alpha_{j+1,T}-\alpha_{j,T})\leq\frac{C_{\kappa}}{\lambda}.

∎

K.2
Stability of AdamW

Lemma 43
.

Let
\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R}}_{k},\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R}}_{k}
be the mean and variance accumulators for the gradients
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{k})
,
g_{i}\in\mathscr{V}
defined in Algorithm
1
for
\mathcal{R}:\mathscr{V}\mapsto\mathscr{V}
either the identity map or given by equation (
26
). Suppose that the constants
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in(0,1)
in Algorithm
1
are such that
\beta_{1}\leq\beta_{2}
. Then, we have

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\frac{(1-\beta_{2}^{j})(1-\beta_{1})}{(1-\beta_{1}^{j})(1-\beta_{2})}}\leq B_{\beta},

where
B_{\beta}:=(1-\beta_{1})^{1/2}(1-\beta_{2})^{-1/2}
.

Proof.

This proof adapts that given in Lemma 4.2 from
[
53
]
.
Write
\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{j}}
and notice that each choice of
\mathcal{R}
is component-wise
1
-homogeneous and sub-additive. Furthermore, the output of
\mathcal{R}
is constant within blocks of
\mathbb{R}^{k\times 4d}
, and so the variance accumulator
\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R}}
is constant within these blocks. As a result, the
1
-homogeneity of
\mathcal{R}
allows the division by the variance accumulators to be factored out, yielding

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{j}}}{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}\left\|\frac{(1-\beta_{1})\mathcal{R}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-i}g_{i}\right)}{\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j}}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{j}}}{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}\left\|\frac{(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-i}\mathcal{R}\left(g_{i}\right)}{\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j}}\right\|_{\infty}.

The final inequality results for
\mathcal{R}
either being the identity or satisfying a componentwise triangle inequality.
Then, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathcal{R}\left(\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}\right)}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}^{j}}}{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}\left\|(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{\beta_{1}^{j-i}\mathcal{R}(g_{i})^{\odot 2}}{\left(\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j}\right)^{\odot 2}}\right\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{i=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-i}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(120)

The final geometric series simplifies to
\sqrt{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}
.
By substituting in the recursion relation for
v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}
given in Algorithm
1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{\beta_{1}^{j-i}(\mathcal{R}(g_{i}))^{\odot 2}}{(\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j})^{\odot 2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-i}\frac{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{i}-\beta_{2}v^{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}}{(\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j})^{\odot 2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{2}}\frac{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}{(\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j})^{\odot 2}}+\frac{1}{1-\beta_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2})\frac{\beta_{1}^{j-i-1}v^{\mathcal{R}}_{i}}{(\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}.

The second equality uses the initial condition
v^{\mathcal{R}}_{0}=0
. Since
(\beta_{1}-\beta_{2})<0
and each component of
v^{\mathcal{R}}_{i}
is greater than zero, the final sum in the penultimate line is componentwise less than or equal to zero. Therefore, substituting the preceding expression into (
120
) gives

\left\|\mathcal{R}\left(\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta_{2}^{j}}{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}}\left\|\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{2}}\frac{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}{\left(\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R}}_{j}}+\varepsilon_{j}\right)^{\odot 2}}\right\|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1-\beta_{2}^{j}}{1-\beta_{1}^{j}}\frac{1-\beta_{1}}{1-\beta_{2}}}.

∎

Lemma 44
.

Given sequences of gradients
G^{(1)}=\{g^{(1)}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{j},G^{(2)}=\{g^{(2)}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{j}
in
\mathscr{V}
, and let
\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(i)}_{j}
,
\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(i)}_{j}
,
i=1,2
be the momentum and variance accumulators defined in equation (
27
) computed using
G^{(i)}
. Then, with
\mathcal{R}
either the identity or given by (
26
), the Adam updates satisfy the Lipchitz estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\varepsilon}-\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1-\beta_{1})}{\varepsilon^{2}(1-\beta_{1}^{j})}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{j}\left(\beta_{1}^{j-i}+2\beta_{2}^{j-i}\right)\Delta^{2}_{i}\right],

where
\Delta_{i}=\|g^{(1)}_{i}-g^{(2)}_{i}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.

Proof.

Since the division in the Adam updates is applied componentwise, adding and subtracting
\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}
divided by
\sqrt{\hat{v}_{j}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}}+\varepsilon
gives

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}{\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\varepsilon}-\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}{\sqrt{v^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}-\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(121)

\displaystyle+\left\|\frac{\left(\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\right)\odot\left(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}-\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\right)}{\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\varepsilon\right)\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}+\varepsilon\right)\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}\right)}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where all multiplication and division is understood to hold componentwise.
The squared Frobenius norm is equal to the sum of the squared Frobenius norms on each block that
\mathcal{R}
and
\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}
are constant on. Write
\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}
for a representative of the
i
-th out of
N(\mathcal{R})
blocks that
\mathcal{R}
is constant on. With this notation, we get that

\displaystyle\Bigg\|

\displaystyle\frac{\big(\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\big)\odot\big(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}-\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\big)}{\big(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\varepsilon\big)\big(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}+\varepsilon\big)\big(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}\big)}\Bigg\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}

(122)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\left\|\frac{\mathcal{R}\left(\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\right)}{\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}+\varepsilon}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N(\mathcal{R})}\frac{\left(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}-\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}\right)^{2}}{\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}}+\varepsilon\right)^{2}\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}}+\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{(1-\beta_{2}^{j})(1-\beta_{1})}{(1-\beta_{1}^{j})(1-\beta_{2})}\sum_{i=1}^{N(\mathcal{R})}\frac{\left(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}-\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}\right)^{2}}{\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}}+\varepsilon\right)^{2}\left(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}}+\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}}\right)^{2}},

where we have used the training step dependent bound in Lemma
43
. Then, writing
g_{\tau,i}
for the
i
-th block of gradients at training step
\tau
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies that each block satisfies the following Lipschitz continuity estimate

\displaystyle\left(v^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}-v^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left((1-\beta_{2})\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{2}^{j-\tau}\left(\|g^{(1)}_{\tau,i}\|-\|g^{(2)}_{\tau,i}\|\right)\left(\|g^{(1)}_{\tau,i}\|+\|g^{(2)}_{\tau,i}\|\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left((1-\beta_{2})\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{2}^{j-\tau}(\|g^{(1)}_{\tau,i}\|-\|g^{(2)}_{\tau,i}\|)^{2}\right)\left((1-\beta_{2})\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{2}^{j-\tau}(\|g^{(1)}_{\tau,i}\|+\|g^{(2)}_{\tau,i}\|)^{2}\right).

Applying the reverse triangle inequality, Young’s inequality and restoring the bias correction by dividing by
(1-\beta_{2}^{j})^{2}
, on the above expression, yields

\displaystyle\left(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}-\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j,i}+\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j,i}\right)\left(\frac{(1-\beta_{2})}{(1-\beta_{2}^{j})}\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{2}^{j-\tau}\|g^{(1)}_{\tau,i}-g^{(2)}_{\tau,i}\|^{2}\right)

(123)

Therefore, by substituting this bound back into equation (
122
) and applying the fact that
(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})^{2}\geq x+y
for
x,y\geq 0
to the denominator, we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg\|

\displaystyle\frac{\big(\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\big)\odot\big(\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}-\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\big)}{\big(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\varepsilon\big)\big(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}+\varepsilon\big)\big(\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}}+\sqrt{\hat{v}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}}\big)}\Bigg\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{2(1-\beta_{1})}{\varepsilon^{2}(1-\beta_{1}^{j})}\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{2}^{j-\tau}\Delta^{2}_{\tau}.

(124)

Furthermore, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(1)}_{j}-\hat{m}^{\mathcal{R},(2)}_{j}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left((1-\beta_{1})\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\frac{\beta_{1}^{j-\tau}}{(1-\beta_{1}^{j})^{2}}\Delta_{\tau}^{2}\right)\left((1-\beta_{1})\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-\tau}\right)

(125)

\displaystyle\leq\left((1-\beta_{1})\sum_{\tau=1}^{j}\beta_{1}^{j-\tau}\Delta_{\tau}^{2}\right)(1-\beta_{1}^{j})^{-1}.

By applying Young’s inequality on equation (
121
) and substituting in bounds (
124
) and (
125
) into the resulting expression gives the desired result.
∎
